  LIKELY FALSE POSITIVES OR LOW SIGNAL
  - Static flat-type-hierarchy (everything at 16px): false positive. The static
  parser does not resolve Tailwind CDN classes such as text-sm, sm:text-base and
  text-xs on h1 (L135), h2 (L215/L253) and h3 (L284). The rendered values
  (12/14/16px) are the real ones, and they are still flat (1.17:1), so the
  rendered finding stands.
  - Leaflet attribution low-contrast: third-party Leaflet markup sampled over
  map tiles. It is 4.49:1 on mobile, essentially at threshold. Low signal.
  - design-system-color hits that are JS map-styling literals passed to Leaflet
  (polyline and circle colors at L1390, L1473, L1567, L1621, and #ffffff at
  L1542): drift relative to DESIGN.md, but they are map data colors rather than
  UI chrome. Advisory only.
  - L108 and L112 (#cbd5e1, #94a3b8): scrollbar styling, low signal.
  - L77 rgba colors: pulse-animation shadow keyframes, cosmetic.
  - design-system-font for JetBrains Mono, Inter and the overused-font rule:
  real findings relative to DESIGN.md. Whether they matter depends on whether
  DESIGN.md is meant to describe this app, which I did not judge.
  - L54 `14px !important` radius: probably an override of Leaflet popup or
  control styling, but I did not confirm this. Real drift either way.
  - Real, not false positives: the contrast warnings on #sim-btn-text (2.1:1),
  #biz-pin-biz-1 (1.9:1), #graph-source-badge and the telemetry labels (2.5:1),
  the 9 to 10px functional text, and the mobile text-occlusion between map pins.
  Map pins are app-authored divIcons (#biz-pin-*), not Leaflet's own markup.

› Message from general-purpose
  **Assessment A: Design Review of app/index.html (GoIA / RutaSegura app)**

  **Screenshots:** both worked, and the OSM tiles loaded.
  - C:\Users\HAB\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\02a07807f892a82a\419318f0-aea3-43b9-b
  490-2d7512e1de6e\scratchpad\critA\1440x900.png
  - C:\Users\HAB\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\02a07807f892a82a\419318f0-aea3-43b9-b
  490-2d7512e1de6e\scratchpad\critA\390x844.png

  On a 390px phone, the two floating toolbars cover about 40% of the 46vh map.
  The route sits squeezed under them. The legend is hidden (`hidden sm:flex`,
  L360).

  **Does the app follow DESIGN.md?** No, and the split looks accidental rather
  than chosen. There is no comment, token or note that marks a deliberate split,
  and the app looks like a dev console built before DESIGN.md. It breaks almost
  every rule:
  - **Fonts:** Inter and JetBrains Mono (L14) instead of Noticia Text and Vend
  Sans.
  - **Name:** the visible brand is "RutaSegura" (L135), not GoIA. That is the
  code name, against PRODUCT.md.
  - **Logo:** a shield icon on an emerald gradient (L128-131). DESIGN.md
  explicitly says no shields.
  - **Route colors:** the safe route is emerald (L1579) and the fast route is
  indigo (L1567/1579). That gives two competing accents, against the One Line
  Rule and the Gray Alternative Rule.
  - **Yellow/amber:** used for sponsored businesses (gold pulsing pins, L1653),
  the "Simular" CTA (L217) and medium-risk zones (L1473). It never marks the
  route.
  - **Risk zones:** alarm red/rose (L1473-1476) instead of Zone Clay.
  - **Shadows:** `shadow-soft/float/gold` everywhere.
  - **Uppercase labels:** small uppercase tracked eyebrows (L186, L215, L283),
  which DESIGN.md bans.
  - **Title tag:** the browser title (L6) reads "Motor A* y Negocios
  Patrocinados".

  ---

  ## Design-specificity verdict

  The UI is category-interchangeable. It is a stock Tailwind slate-and-emerald
  "AI dashboard" (gradient logo tile, emoji-prefixed buttons, KPI tiles, dark
  "analysis" card, glowing pins). Swap the street names and it could be any
  routing demo.

  The only parts written for this product are the data: real Río-street
  intersections, the Reforma corridor, and named safe points. The visual layer
  is authored for a grader of an A* assignment, not for a walker in Cuauhtémoc.

  ---

  ## Nielsen heuristics (total 15/40, poor band)

  1. **Visibility of system status: 2.** Toasts and active mode states exist.
  But if `runAStarSearch` returns null, `renderSidebarMetricsAndDiagnostics`
  just returns early (L1740), so the old metrics stay on screen. The OSM load
  has no spinner, only a toast (L2153).
  2. **Match with the real world: 1.** The core panel speaks algorithm, not
  street:
     - formula `h_seg(n)=…` (L203)
     - "Prioridad del Algoritmo A*" (L186) and "Telemetría en Vivo · Búsqueda
  A*" (L215)
     - "Nodos A*" and "Cómputo ms" (L237-242), "Nodos A* (+Δh%)" (L354)
     - "Riesgo Alto (α×2.8)" (L392)
     - "Overpass API ocupado" (L2243)
     - raw coordinates as place labels, "Origen (19.4295, -99.1665)" (L2014)
  3. **User control and freedom: 2.** Modals have Cancel and the swap button
  exists. But "Limpiar riesgos/negocios" (L297-304) and "Demo" (L153) wipe state
  with no confirm or undo. There is no Esc on modals.
  4. **Consistency and standards: 2.** Colors mean several things at once:
     - Green: safe route, origin A, "Prioridad Seguridad", success toast,
  distance-to-route pill.
     - Amber: sponsors, the simulate CTA, medium risk.
     - Indigo: fast route, the compare toggle, the walker marker.
     - Buttons are Title Case ("Fijar Destino", "Llegar Rápido"), which is not
  standard Spanish. The copy mixes tú ("Prueba", L1775) and usted ("su radio").
  5. **Error prevention: 1.**
     - The default click mode is "origin" (L844), so any stray tap moves A and
  recalculates.
     - Risk circles are L.circle with the default `bubblingMouseEvents:true`.
  Clicking a zone to read it probably also fires the map click and moves the
  origin there (L1471/L2013). This should be verified.
     - The risk form is prefilled with "Calle con poca iluminación nocturna"
  (L409), so a one-click save creates a report with fabricated wording.
  6. **Recognition rather than recall: 2.** Most controls have text labels. But
  there is no address search, no recent places, the legend is hidden on mobile,
  and on mobile the OSM button is emoji-only (L150-151).
  7. **Flexibility and efficiency: 2.** Markers can be dragged and the swap is
  quick. There is no typed search, no keyboard map path, and no "use my
  location".
  8. **Aesthetic and minimalist design: 1.** About 15 competing chips, tiles and
  badges sit above the fold. The pulsing gold ad pins (L92-94, infinite) are
  the loudest objects on the map, louder than the route. OSM's red hospital
  crosses add noise.
  9. **Error recovery: 1.** "No route found" is silent. The OSM error is jargon
  with no next step. Pins dropped outside the colonia snap silently to the
  nearest node, and a straight segment is drawn through buildings (L1221-1228).
  10. **Help and documentation: 1.** Only the empty business list has a hint
  (L1775). Nothing explains what "Seguridad 97%" means or how the score is
  computed.

  ---

  ## Cognitive load: 7 of 8 checklist items fail (high load)

  Only grouping passes.

  - **Single focus:** fails. Walker, sponsor-admin and researcher tasks share
  one screen.
  - **Chunking:** fails. The metric row has 5 tiles (L223).
  - **Visual hierarchy:** fails. The amber simulate button and the dark
  diagnostics card outweigh the route.
  - **One thing at a time:** fails.
  - **Minimal choices:** fails. The map toolbar has 6 options (4 modes and 2
  toggles, L318-355). The sidebar header plus the priority area has another 5
  (OSM, Demo, swap, 2 priorities).
  - **Working memory:** fails. To compare times, the user must switch priority
  and remember the earlier number. `altRoute` time is computed but never shown
  as text, only as a dashed line (L1564).
  - **Progressive disclosure:** fails. The formula and node telemetry are always
  visible.

  ---

  ## Emotional journey

  - **Entry:** a dense console with a policeman-shield logo and red zones. It
  feels like a crime dashboard, not a shaded sidewalk.
  - **Peak:** the route draws instantly. This is good.
  - **Valley:** the user cannot answer "how much longer is the safe way?"
  without hunting for it.
  - **High-stakes moment:** reporting a zone uses ⚠️, rose-red "Riesgo Alto" and
  the "Guardar y Recalcular A*" button (L417). It is alarming and technical at
  the same moment the user is anxious.
  - **Moment of reassurance:** the diagnostics line "Evita el 100% de las zonas
  inseguras" or "Respaldada por 3 puntos seguros" (L1295-1305). It is buried in
  the 3rd card, below the ads.
  - **End:** "🏁 ¡Has llegado…!" only appears inside a simulation (L1922), and
  while walking the sim fires ad toasts (L1940). So the end of the journey
  belongs to the sponsors.

  ---

  ## Strengths

  1. The A/B card with labelled endpoints and a swap button (L164-181) is
  compact and clear.
  2. The plain-language route diagnostics (L1294-1310) name the zone crossed and
  the safe points used. This is the seed of the "why this route" explanation
  the brand needs.
  3. Sponsors are listed in walking order with their distance to the route
  (L1766-1768). Pins far from the route are muted (L1673). The concept fits the
  business model well.

  ---

  ## Priority issues

  **[P0] The core promise is missing: there is no safe-vs-fast time
  comparison.**
  - *Why it matters:* Product Principle 1 ("always show what the safer route
  costs in time") is the whole product. Today the user sees only the active
  route's 12 min. Also, the safe and fast routes are often identical in the
  demo, so the dashed comparison line is invisible under the main line.
  - *Fix:* put a pair of pills at the top of the result: "Ruta GoIA · 12 min"
  (yellow) and "Más rápida · 10 min" (gray), plus the +N min delta. Add an
  explicit "Ambas rutas coinciden" state when they are the same. Recolor the
  routes to yellow solid and gray dotted.
  - *Command:* /impeccable clarify, then colorize.

  **[P1] The walker app, sponsor admin and algorithm lab are fused into one
  screen.**
  - *Why it matters:* the formula, Nodos A*, Cómputo, OSM/Demo, "Agregar
  Negocio" and Oro/Básico plan radii are noise to the primary user. They also
  break the "never technical" commitment.
  - *Fix:* the default view shows only A/B, the route pair, the safety
  explanation and "Reportar". Move the A* telemetry, the formula and the
  explored nodes into a collapsed "Modo técnico" or `?debug`. Move business CRUD
  and the clear buttons to a separate admin view.
  - *Command:* /impeccable distill.

  **[P1] The color and emotional system contradicts DESIGN.md and is
  alarm-toned.**
  - *Why it matters:* red zones, the shield logo, police emoji and pulsing gold
  ads read as crime news and advertising, not calm reassurance. Ads currently
  outshine the route.
  - *Fix:*
    - Apply the DESIGN.md tokens: forest ink, Daylight Ground, Noticia/Vend
  Sans, clay zones with low-alpha fill.
    - Reserve yellow for the route.
    - Turn sponsor pins into quiet ink or neutral pins, and kill the infinite
  `gold-pin-active` glow.
    - Rename the brand to GoIA.
    - Add `prefers-reduced-motion` handling.
  - *Command:* /impeccable colorize, then quieter, then typeset.

  **[P1] Map input is error-prone and gives no search.**
  - *Why it matters:* the default click mode silently moves the origin,
  risk-zone clicks probably move it too, and there is no way to type "Metro
  Insurgentes".
  - *Fix:*
    - Add autocomplete A/B fields (even a fixed list of local landmarks).
    - Make the idle map click inert, or open a "¿Salir desde aquí / Llegar
  aquí?" popover.
    - Set `bubblingMouseEvents:false` on risk circles.
    - Reverse-label dropped pins with the nearest intersection name from the
  graph (it is already in `node.name`) instead of coordinates.
  - *Command:* /impeccable harden.

  **[P2] Accessibility and mobile layout.**
  - *Issues:*
    - Labels are not linked to their inputs (L390, 397, 408, 437…).
    - The ✕ close buttons and the mobile OSM button have no accessible name.
    - Modals have no role="dialog", no focus trap and no Esc.
    - The toast container has no `aria-live` (L483).
    - Text is 9-10px throughout (L136, L225).
    - Metric labels are slate-400 on slate-50, about 2.9:1 contrast.
    - On mobile, toolbars cover the map and the legend is hidden.
  - *Fix:*
    - Raise the type floor to 12-13px.
    - Associate the labels and add aria-labels.
    - On phone, collapse the toolbars into a bottom sheet with A/B and the route
  pair inside the thumb zone.
  - *Commands:* /impeccable adapt and /impeccable audit.

  ---

  ## Persona red flags

  - **Jordan (first-timer):** there is no obvious first action. "Fijar Origen
  (A)" is already active, so nothing tells them to tap the map. "Seguridad 97%"
  is unexplained. α, Φ and Δh% are jargon. There is no confirmation that a
  report was received beyond "Heurística A* actualizada".
  - **Casey (mobile, one hand):** every control is at the top of the screen, out
  of thumb reach. Targets are about 28px (py-1.5 text-[11px]). The map is only
  46vh and half covered. Nothing persists on reload. Tiles plus the Tailwind CDN
  make for a heavy first load.
  - **Sam (accessibility):** the map modes and markers are mouse-only. Risk
  level is conveyed by red vs amber alone; dashed vs solid helps only slightly.
  Unlabelled fields, toasts never announced, emoji used as icons and read aloud.
  - **Riley (stress tester):**
    - The "Limpiar" buttons wipe with no undo.
    - A pin outside the polygon gives a straight line through blocks.
    - A route that is not found leaves stale numbers.
    - Business names are injected with innerHTML, which is an XSS path (L1651,
  L1795, L1941).
    - The "Desc." field is prefilled, so empty saves create plausible fake
  reports.
  - **Project persona "Mariana", grad student new to CDMX walking from Metro
  Insurgentes to campus on a phone:**
    - She cannot search "Metro Insurgentes".
    - She cannot see "+3 min for the safe route".
    - Red circles and police emoji raise her anxiety rather than lowering it.
    - Sponsor promos ("2×1 espresso") sit above the answer to "is this street
  OK?". That ordering signals the app serves advertisers first.
  - **Project persona "Don Ernesto", sponsoring café owner:** the plan radii are
  visible, but he would never have access to this walker screen. Admin and
  consumer views are confused.

  ---

  ## Minor observations

  - The default "Seguridad" value is clamped to 18-99 with a made-up base of 94
  (L1286). Showing "97%" implies a precision the model does not have, which
  conflicts with "Claim only what is true". Consider words (alta/media) plus the
  reason.
  - The boundary polygon (teal dashed) adds yet another hue.
  - "Ver / Editar" in the walker's sponsor card is an admin action shown to
  walkers.
  - The sim walker emits a sponsor toast every 150ms tick check. On a real walk
  this would be spammy.
  - The tile attribution is overlapped by the legend at 1440px.
  - The `<title>` should be "GoIA".

  ---

  ## Provocative questions

  1. If the safe and fast routes coincide (as in the demo default), what does
  the app say? Isn't "this is already the safest and fastest way" the most
  reassuring message you could show?
  2. Who is this screen for: a walker, a sponsor or a professor? Could you
  defend showing the A* formula to Mariana at 9 pm outside Metro Insurgentes?
  3. Should a sponsor ever be visually louder than the route? If the gold pulse
  were removed, would the business model still work?

● Method: dual-agent (A: design review · B: detector + rendered scan)

  │       │ real world      │       │ "α×2.8") and labels places with raw   │

  Design specificity verdict

  Design review: you could put this UI on any product. It's the stock Tailwind
  slate-and-emerald "AI dashboard": a gradient shield logo, buttons with emoji
  in front, KPI tiles, a dark analysis card and glowing pins. The only parts
  specific to this product are the data (the Río-street intersections, the
  Reforma corridor, the named safe points). The visual layer looks built for
  whoever grades an A* assignment, not for someone walking through Cuauhtémoc.

  It also ignores the project's own DESIGN.md, and nothing marks that as
  deliberate:
  - It uses Inter and JetBrains Mono instead of Noticia Text and Vend Sans.
  - The name shown is "RutaSegura", not GoIA.
  - The logo is a shield, which DESIGN.md bans by name.
  - The safe route is emerald and the fast route is indigo. DESIGN.md says
    yellow for the route and gray for the faster alternative.
  - Risk zones are alarm red.
  - It uses drop shadows and uppercase eyebrow labels, which DESIGN.md also
    bans.

  Detector:
  - Static scan: 22 findings. 12 colors and 3 fonts aren't in DESIGN.md, and one
    14px radius drifts from the scale.
  - Rendered scan: 36 findings at 1440px and 35 at 390px. The ones that matter:
    - About 15 UI labels render at 9 to 10px: the telemetry labels, the
      "ORO"/"BÁSICO" badges, "α=1.35" and "Ver / Editar".
    - Real contrast failures:
      - "Simular Paso por Ruta" button: 2.1:1
      - gold sponsor pin: 1.9:1
      - telemetry labels: 2.5:1
    - On phone, two sponsor-pin labels overlap.
    - The headings barely step up in size (12, 14 and 16px).
  - Both reviews flag the off-brand fonts and colors. The detector also caught
    the pin overlap on phone, which the design review missed.
  - Likely false positives:
    - the static "everything is 16px" finding (the parser can't resolve Tailwind
      CDN classes)
    - the contrast of Leaflet's own attribution link
    - the scrollbar colors

  Overlays: none. There was no interactive browser, so the scans and screenshots
  only cover the initial load.

  Overall impression

  The route engine and the data are the strong part. The screen is three
  products stacked together: a walker app, a sponsor admin panel and an A* lab.
  The walker (the actual user) gets the least room. The biggest opportunity is
  the one comparison the whole product exists for: safe route versus fast route,
  in minutes, side by side. Right now it isn't there.

  What's working

  1. The A/B card (index.html:164-181 (app/index.html#L164-L181)): labelled
     endpoints and a swap button. It's compact and clear.
  2. The plain-language diagnostics (index.html:1294-1310
     (app/index.html#L1294-L1310)): "Cruza 1 zona de riesgo… Respaldada por 3
     puntos seguros". This is the start of a "why this route" explanation, which
     is exactly the trust signal the product needs.
  3. Sponsors are ordered by walking order and distance to the route. Pins far
     from the route are muted. This fits the business model better than plain
     ads would.

  Priority issues

  [P0] The safe-vs-fast comparison is missing.
  - Why it matters: "show what the safer route costs in time" is the product's
    first principle. state.altRoute is computed (index.html:1857
    (app/index.html#L1857)) but only drawn as a dashed line (index.html:1564
    (app/index.html#L1564)). Its time never appears as text, and in the demo the
    two routes coincide, so the dashed line sits hidden under the main one.
  - Fix: put two pills at the top of the result: "Ruta segura · 12 min" and "Más
    rápida · 10 min", with the +N min difference. When the two coincide, say
    "Ambas rutas coinciden". Draw the routes as solid yellow and dotted gray.
  - Command: /impeccable clarify, then /impeccable colorize.

  [P1] Walker, admin and lab are fused into one screen.
  - Why it matters: the formula, "Nodos A*", "Cómputo", OSM/Demo, "Agregar
    Negocio" and the Oro/Básico radii are noise to someone trying to get to
    campus.
  - Fix: the default view shows only A/B, the route pair, the "why" text and
    "Reportar". Put the telemetry and the formula behind a collapsed "Modo
    técnico" (or ?debug). Move business editing and the clear buttons to an
    admin view.
  - Command: /impeccable distill.

  [P1] The tone is alarmist, and sponsors outshine the route.
  - Why it matters: red zones, a shield logo, police emoji and infinitely
    pulsing gold pins read as crime news plus advertising. During the
    simulation, the ending belongs to the sponsors too (ad toasts while
    walking).
  - Fix: apply the DESIGN.md tokens: forest ink, clay zones with a low-opacity
    fill, Noticia and Vend Sans. Keep yellow for the route only. Make sponsor
    pins quiet and remove the glow. Add prefers-reduced-motion (there is none at
    all today).
  - Command: /impeccable colorize, /impeccable quieter, /impeccable typeset.

  [P1] Map input is error-prone and there's no search.
  - Why it matters: a stray tap moves the origin. Clicks on risk circles
    probably fall through to the map, because Leaflet's default
    bubblingMouseEvents is on and nothing overrides it (index.html:1471
    (app/index.html#L1471)). And nobody can type "Metro Insurgentes".
  - Fix:
    - Add A/B fields with autocomplete over a fixed list of landmarks.
    - Make an idle map tap open "¿Salir desde aquí / Llegar aquí?" instead of
      moving A.
    - Set bubblingMouseEvents: false on the risk circles.
    - Label dropped pins with the nearest node.name instead of coordinates.
  - Command: /impeccable harden.

  [P2] Accessibility and the phone layout.
  - Issues:
    - Text runs 9 to 10px.
    - Contrast fails, as listed above.
    - Labels aren't linked to their inputs.
    - The ✕ buttons have no accessible name.
    - Modals have no dialog role, focus trap or Esc.
    - Toasts aren't announced to screen readers (no aria-live).
    - On a 390px phone, the two floating toolbars cover about 40% of the 46vh
      map, and every control sits at the top, out of thumb reach.
  - Fix: set the minimum text size at 12px. Fix the labels and dialog semantics.
    On phone, move A/B and the route pair into a bottom sheet.
  - Command: /impeccable adapt, /impeccable audit.

  Persona red flags

  - First-timer: "Fijar Origen (A)" is already active, so nothing tells them to
    tap the map. "Seguridad 97%", α, Φ and Δh% go unexplained. After a report,
    the only confirmation is "Heurística A* actualizada".
  - One-handed phone user: every control is at the top of the screen. Tap
    targets are about 28px. Nothing survives a reload.
  - Screen-reader user: the map modes are mouse-only. Risk level is shown by red
    vs amber only. Emoji are used as icons and get read aloud.
  - Stress tester:
    - "Limpiar" has no undo.
    - A pin dropped outside the polygon gets a straight line drawn through
      buildings.
    - Business names go in through innerHTML (index.html:1651
      (app/index.html#L1651)), which opens an XSS path.
    - The prefilled "Desc." text lets an empty save create a plausible fake
      report.
  - New grad student, Metro Insurgentes to campus:
    - They can't search the metro station.
    - They can't see "+3 min for the safe route".
    - They read a "2×1 espresso" promo before they learn whether the street is
      OK.

  Minor observations

  - The "97%" safety score comes from a made-up base of 94, clamped to 18-99
    (index.html:1286 (app/index.html#L1286)). It suggests precision the model
    doesn't have. "Alta" plus the reason would be more honest.
  - The <title> reads "Motor A* y Negocios Patrocinados".
  - The teal dashed boundary polygon adds another hue.
  - The legend overlaps the tile attribution at 1440px.
  - The Spanish mixes tú and usted, and the button labels use English-style
    Title Case.

  Questions to consider

  - When the safe and fast routes coincide, isn't "this is already the safest
    and fastest way" the most reassuring thing the app could say?
  - Who is this screen for: a walker, a sponsor or the professor? If the answer
    is the professor, the telemetry belongs in a technical mode, not the default
    view.
  - Should a sponsor ever be louder than the route?

  One caveat from me: since this is a course project, the A* telemetry may be on
  screen on purpose for the grader. If so, P1 #2 becomes "hide it behind a
  toggle", not "remove it". That's what the second question below is asking.

  ⎿  Interrupted · What should Claude do instead?

> The goal is that the application should look like an actual application
  rather than just a demo. That means the ability to navigate the interface to
  select a route and submit reports.

● That changes what we fix first. The critique scored how the demo looks, but
  your actual gap is that the prototype has no real user flows. Today everything
  happens by tapping the map in different modes, which is why it feels like a
  demo. A real app would be built around these screens:

  1. Home: the map with your location and one "¿A dónde vas?" search bar at the
     bottom. No toolbars, no telemetry.
  2. Choose the route: fields for where you start and where you're going, with
     autocomplete over the landmarks and intersections in the graph (Metro
     Insurgentes, Ángel, and so on). You can still drop a pin on the map as a
     fallback.
  3. Pick a route: a bottom sheet with two route cards. The safe route (yellow,
     "12 min") sits next to the fastest one (gray, "10 min · cruza 1 zona").
     Each card has a "why" line and an "Iniciar" button.
  4. Navigate: the next street name, the time remaining and an "En ruta segura"
     status, with the walker moving along the route. A "Reportar" button is
     always visible, and it ends on an arrival screen.
  5. Report: you pick a category (poca luz, asalto, acoso, banqueta rota…),
     confirm the location (your current spot or a map pin), add an optional note
     and submit. You then see "Reporte enviado" and the route recalculates.
     Your reports are saved in the browser and listed in a "Mis reportes" page.
  6. The rest moves into menus: sponsor businesses appear as quiet stops on the
     route, and the A* telemetry goes behind "Modo técnico" in a menu. Business
     editing moves to an admin page.

  All of this would follow DESIGN.md, which also takes care of the tone, the
  phone layout and the accessibility issues from the critique. Before I start,
  there are two things I can't settle myself:

       557 +function haversineMeters(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2) {           
       558 +  const R = 6371000;                                         
       559 +  const toRad = Math.PI / 180;                               
       560 +  const dLat = (lat2 - lat1) * toRad;                        
       561 +  const dLon = (lon2 - lon1) * toRad;                        
       562 +  const a = Math.sin(dLat / 2) * Math.sin(dLat / 2) +        
       563 +    Math.cos(lat1 * toRad) * Math.cos(lat2 * toRad) * Math.si
           +n(dLon / 2) * Math.sin(dLon / 2);                            
       564 +  return 2 * R * Math.atan2(Math.sqrt(a), Math.sqrt(1 - a)); 
       565 +}

       567 +function pointToRoute(lat, lng, coords) {                    
       568 +  if (!coords || coords.length < 2) return { distance: Infini
           +ty, progress: 0 };                                           
       569 +  const mLat = 110540;                                       
       570 +  const mLng = 111320 * Math.cos(lat * Math.PI / 180);       
       571 +  let best = Infinity;                                       
       572 +  let bestProgress = 0;                                      
       573 +  let cumulative = 0;                                        
       574 +  for (let i = 0; i < coords.length - 1; i++) {              
       575 +    const ax = (coords[i][1] - lng) * mLng;                  
       576 +    const ay = (coords[i][0] - lat) * mLat;                  
       577 +    const bx = (coords[i + 1][1] - lng) * mLng;              
       578 +    const by = (coords[i + 1][0] - lat) * mLat;              
       579 +    const dx = bx - ax;                                      
       580 +    const dy = by - ay;                                      
       581 +    const lenSq = dx * dx + dy * dy;                         
       582 +    let t = 0;                                               
       583 +    if (lenSq > 1e-9) t = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, -(ax * dx +
           + ay * dy) / lenSq));                                         
       584 +    const qx = ax + t * dx;                                  
       585 +    const qy = ay + t * dy;                                  
       586 +    const d = Math.sqrt(qx * qx + qy * qy);                  
       587 +    if (d < best) {                                          
       588 +      best = d;                                              
       589 +      bestProgress = cumulative + t * Math.sqrt(lenSq);      
       590 +    }                                                        
       591 +    cumulative += Math.sqrt(lenSq);                          
       592 +  }                                                          
       593 +  return { distance: Math.round(best), progress: bestProgress
           + };                                                          
       594 +}

           +xi) * (lat - yi) / (yj - yi) + xi;                           
       602 +    if (crosses) inside = !inside;                           
       603 +  }                                                          
       604 +  return inside;

      1524 +function bearing(a, b) {                                     
      1525 +  const toRad = Math.PI / 180;                               
      1526 +  const y = Math.sin((b[1] - a[1]) * toRad) * Math.cos(b[0] *
           + toRad);                                                     
      1527 +  const x = Math.cos(a[0] * toRad) * Math.sin(b[0] * toRad) -
           + Math.sin(a[0] * toRad) * Math.cos(b[0] * toRad) * Math.cos((
           +b[1] - a[1]) * toRad);                                       
      1528 +  return Math.atan2(y, x) * 180 / Math.PI;                   
      1529 +}

      1531 +function positionAt(p) {                                     
      1532 +  const nav = state.nav;                                     
      1533 +  const c = nav.coords;                                      
      1534 +  for (let i = 0; i < c.length - 1; i++) {                   
      1535 +    if (p <= nav.cum[i + 1]) {                               
      1536 +      const seg = nav.cum[i + 1] - nav.cum[i];               
      1537 +      const t = seg > 0 ? (p - nav.cum[i]) / seg : 0;        
      1538 +      return [c[i][0] + (c[i + 1][0] - c[i][0]) * t, c[i][1] 
           ++ (c[i + 1][1] - c[i][1]) * t];                              
      1539      }
      1540 +  }                                                          
      1541 +  return c[c.length - 1];                                    
      1542 +}

      1600 +function tick() {                                            
      1601 +  const nav = state.nav;                                     
      1602 +  if (!nav || nav.paused) return;                            
      1603 +  nav.p = Math.min(nav.total, nav.p + SIM_STEP_M);           
      1604 +  const pos = positionAt(nav.p);                             
      1605 +  if (walkerMarker) walkerMarker.setLatLng(pos);             
      1606 +  if (!map.getBounds().pad(-0.3).contains(pos)) map.panTo(pos
           +, { animate: true, duration: 0.5 });                         
      1607 +  for (const item of nav.bizOnRoute) {                       
      1608 +    const b = item.biz;                                      
      1609 +    if (nav.seenBiz.indexOf(b.id) === -1 && haversineMeters(p
           +os[0], pos[1], b.lat, b.lng) <= tierOf(b.tier).radius) {     
      1610 +      nav.seenBiz.push(b.id);                                
      1611 +      nav.notice = b;

      1613 +      if (box) delete box.dataset.id;                        
      1614      }

      1616 +  updateNavUI();                                             
      1617 +  if (nav.p >= nav.total) arrive();                          
      1618 +}

● I rebuilt app/index.html as a working app. You can now search for a place,
  compare two routes, walk one with turn-by-turn directions, and submit reports,
  and the routes change in response to those reports. I drove every flow in a
  headless browser at desktop and phone size and got no script errors.

  Backup: app/ was never committed to git, so the original prototype is saved as
  app/prototipo-v0.html. Delete it once you've committed the new version.

  What a user can do now:
  - Home: a "¿A dónde caminas hoy?" search bar, recent destinations, a few
    landmarks, and a "Reportar algo cerca de mí" button. Tapping the map gives
    you "Ir aquí" or "Salir desde aquí".
  - Plan: "Desde" and "Hasta" fields with autocomplete over landmarks, sponsor
    businesses and every corner in the graph. You can also choose a point by
    dragging the map under a fixed pin, and swap the two endpoints.
  - Routes: this is the comparison the old prototype was missing. The two times
    appear side by side ("Ruta GoIA · 13 min" next to "Más rápida · 12 min"),
    with a plain sentence such as "La ruta GoIA tarda 1 min más y evita una zona
    con reportes." Below that are two selectable routes, each with a short
    reason, and the sponsors along the chosen one, labelled "Patrocinado".
  - Walk: a simulated walk with directions like "Gira a la izquierda en Río Rhin
    · en 90 m". The sheet shows minutes left and arrival time, a "Reportar"
    button, pause, and "Terminar" (which asks for confirmation). It ends on an
    arrival screen.
  - Report: pick one of six categories, confirm or move the location, say when
    it happened, and add an optional note. You can't send it without a category.
    If you report while walking, the route is recalculated from where you are.
    Reports are stored in this browser, and "Mis reportes" lets you delete one,
    with an undo.
  - Menu:
    - the business admin (add, edit or remove a business, choosing its location
      on the map)
    - "Modo técnico", which shows explored nodes, compute time, the two
      heuristic formulas, and an overlay of explored nodes on the map
    - reset to the sample data

  Changes and choices you should know about:
  - A is untouched.* Both heuristics, the costs and α, β, γ and v_max are
    exactly as they were. What's new is the interface, plus the turn directions
    (worked out from the angle between consecutive street segments) and the
    rerouting after a report.
  - I moved the simulated start point from the Ángel to Río Pánuco and Río
    Tiber. From the Ángel, both searches return the same path to every landmark,
    so the demo could never show a choice. From the new point, the trip to
    Monumento a la Madre shows two different routes.
  - When both searches return the same path, or the "fast" one is actually
    longer, the app shows one route and says so. That avoids a "Más rápida"
    label that takes more minutes.
  - I dropped "Seguridad 97%". The number came from a made-up base value, so the
    app now gives reasons instead.
  - The map uses standard OpenStreetMap tiles, desaturated with CSS so the
    yellow route stays the most prominent thing. CARTO's quieter tiles now need
    an API key.
  - The look follows DESIGN.md: the GoIA name, Noticia Text and Vend Sans
    (copied into app/assets/fonts/), yellow for the route only, gray dots for
    the faster route, clay instead of red for report zones, no drop shadows, and
    drawn icons instead of emoji.
  - I didn't add live Overpass loading. It was a developer feature with no place
    in the walker's flow.

  Still open:
  - PRODUCT.md's Stack section still describes the old app (Tailwind and Inter).
    I didn't edit it.
  - I didn't re-run /impeccable critique, so I don't have a new score to compare
    against the 15/40.

  To run it, serve the folder and open it: python -m http.server --directory
  app. Opening the file directly loses the fonts in some browsers.

  Before you present this, make sure you can explain where the two route times
  come from. Both are distance divided by 80 m/min, while the "fast" search
  itself minimizes a time cost that weights avenues and risk. Be ready to say
  how a report becomes a zone that changes Φ(n): it gets a 70 m radius, and
  "asalto" and "acoso" count as high severity. Those are the parts a grader is
  most likely to ask about.

> openstreetmap 403 access blocked. Referer is not following the tile usage...

● That 403 is OpenStreetMap's tile policy. Their servers now reject requests
  that arrive without a Referer header, and a page opened straight from disk
  (file://, or a preview pane in the editor) never sends one. My tests served
  the page over http://127.0.0.1, which is why they passed. I'll check which
  free tile servers accept requests with no referer at all.

● Bash(for u in "https://tile.openstreetmap.org/16/14720/29195.png"
      "https://server.arcgisonline.com/ArcGIS/rest/services/Canvas/World_Light_G
      ray_Base/MapServer/tile/1…)

● The landing page and the app are now one application in web/, served by one
  Flask server:

  ┌──────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │       URL        │                       File                        │
  ├──────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ /                │ web/index.html: the landing page                  │
  ├──────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ /app/            │ web/app/index.html: the app (/app redirects here) │
  ├──────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ /assets/…        │ shared fonts, images and scripts                  │
  ├──────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ /api/preregistro │ unchanged                                         │
  └──────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  To run it: pip install -r web/requirements.txt, then python web/server.py,
  then open http://127.0.0.1:8000.

  Changes:
  - The landing page leads into the app with a secondary "Probar el demo" link
    in three places: the header (left of "Cómo funciona"), the footer, and after
    "Mientras, prueba el demo." in the pre-registration success message. The
    main call to action is still pre-registration, so H6 still measures
    sign-ups. On phones the header shows only the Pre-registro button, as
    before, so there the demo is reached from the footer or the success message.
  - The app's menu now has "Conoce GoIA", which goes back to the landing page.
  - The fonts are stored once, in web/assets/fonts/. The app had its own copy of
    the same files.
  - The OpenStreetMap 403 is gone as long as you open the app through the
    server. Over http the browser sends a referer, and the tiles loaded in every
    test. Opening the HTML file directly from disk will still get blocked.
  - PRODUCT.md now describes the new layout.

  Checked through the running server:
  - the landing page at desktop and phone size
  - the demo links
  - a real pre-registration, including the link in the success message
  - the jump into the app

    undo, choosing a point on the map)

Two shapes: (1) an element with its own text where the space between the rendered text and the border box is too small for the font size, and (2) a wrapper whose children's text lands flush against a visible boundary (border, outline, or non-transparent background) with nothing to inset it. ● A few things in the measurements need verification before they go in the
  report.
  - Contrast: the warning text on routes measures 4.45:1. That's the clay color
    on white, just under the 4.5:1 AA threshold.
  - Radio buttons: the arrow keys don't move the route choice.
  - Search fields: they don't announce their results list.
  - Landmarks: the app's main content sits in an <aside>, while <main> holds
    only the map.

  None of the app states overflowed horizontally, even with text at 200%.

  Method: detector on both source files plus rendered scans of / and /app/ at
  1440×900 and 390×844. Measurements were taken in headless Chromium, using
  emulated viewports with touch enabled, across six app states. Real touch
  gestures weren't tested.

  Audit Health Score: web/ (landing + app)

  ┌───────┬──────────────────┬───────┬──────────────────────────────────────┐

  ├───────┼──────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤

  │ 1     │ Accessibility    │ 3     │ below AA. The route options don't    │
  │       │                  │       │ respond to arrow keys.               │
  ├───────┼──────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
  │       │                  │       │ The landing hero is a 7.3 MB PNG. A  │
  │ 2     │ Performance      │ 2     │ 489 KB JPEG of the same photo exists │
  │       │                  │       │  and is unused.                      │
  ├───────┼──────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤

  Implementation Integrity Verdict: pass

  Both pages express the same product-specific system:
  - the yellow route paired with the gray faster route
  - Noticia Text and Vend Sans from one shared font folder
  - clay zones
  - no shadows

  The detector found no warnings in either rendered page except one
  cramped-padding warning on the Leaflet map credit, which is third-party
  markup. Its static findings:
  - 13 type sizes off the DESIGN.md ramp. That ramp was written for landing-page
    scale, so app sizes like .92rem and .78rem are legitimate additions that
    aren't documented yet.
  - Colors and radii not in DESIGN.md: #6f706a (the selected fast route),
    #8f908b, the footer's rgb(174,189,179), and radii of 0.45cqw and 3px.
  - One cramped-padding warning on the landing <section>. This is a false
    positive. The static parser doesn't see the .wrap inset, and both rendered
    scans pass.
  - Stragglers (a single word on a paragraph's last line): 6 advisories,
    cosmetic.

  Executive summary

  - Score: 14/20 (Good). 2 P1, 6 P2, 3 P3.
  - Top issues: the 7.3 MB hero image, the clay warning-text contrast, the
    landing map's canvas redrawing nonstop, keyboard handling of the route
    options, and the search field semantics.
  - Next: /impeccable optimize for the landing page's two performance problems,
    then /impeccable harden for the app's accessibility gaps.

  Findings

  [P1] The hero image is a 7.3 MB PNG
  - Location: web/index.html:207 (web/index.html#L207)
  - Category: Performance
  - Impact: This is the first large image on the page. On a phone connection it
    takes several seconds to appear, right where the page has to earn the
    pre-registration (H6). assets/plates/hero-photo.jpg is 489 KB at 2064×1152,
    which is still sharper than any screen it's shown on.
  - Fix: Point src at the JPEG, or serve both through srcset. Remove the PNG
    from the deployed folder.
  - Command: /impeccable optimize

  [P1] Clay warning text fails AA contrast
  - Location: .why .warn in web/app/index.html (the "Pasa cerca de una zona con
    reportes" line)
  - Category: Accessibility, WCAG 1.4.3
  - Impact: At 15px, #a26650 on #fbfbf7 measures 4.45:1 against the required
    4.5:1. This is the exact line that tells someone why the faster route is
    riskier.
  - Fix: Keep the clay color on the icon and set the text in --ink, or darken
    the clay used for text to about #9a5f4a.
  - Command: /impeccable harden

  [P2] The landing map redraws every frame, forever
  - Location: tick() at web/index.html:839 (web/index.html#L839)
  - Category: Performance
  - Impact: The full canvas is repainted continuously: streets, the leaf-light
    texture and 46 leaves. That happens while the map is scrolled out of view,
    and also under reduced motion, where the lens never moves. On phones this
    drains battery while people read the rest of the page.
  - Fix: Start and stop the loop with an IntersectionObserver. Under reduced
    motion, draw one frame and redraw only on pointermove.
  - Command: /impeccable optimize

  [P2] The route options and other radio groups ignore arrow keys
  - Location: routeOption, the report category tiles, "¿Cuándo?", and the
    business plan control in web/app/index.html
  - Category: Accessibility, ARIA radio pattern
  - Impact: They are declared role="radio", but every option is a separate Tab
    stop and ArrowDown does nothing (verified). Screen-reader users expect arrow
    keys to move the choice inside a radio group.
  - Fix: Use roving tabindex with arrow-key handling, or switch to native <input
    type="radio"> styled as tiles.
  - Command: /impeccable harden

  [P2] The search fields don't announce their results
  - Location: #in-from and #in-to in web/app/index.html
  - Category: Accessibility, WCAG 4.1.2
  - Impact: The results list updates as you type, but there is no
    role="combobox", aria-controls or aria-expanded, and no result count is
    announced. A screen-reader user types and hears nothing.
  - Fix: Implement the combobox pattern, or at least add a polite live region
    saying "N resultados".
  - Command: /impeccable harden

  [P2] The app's landmarks are inverted
  - Location: web/app/index.html: <aside class="panel"> and <main
    aria-label="Mapa">
  - Category: Accessibility, WCAG 1.3.1
  - Impact: Every task happens in the panel, but it's marked as an aside. A
    "jump to main" lands on the map, which has nothing keyboard-usable.
  - Fix: Make the panel <main> and the map a labelled <section>.
  - Command: /impeccable harden

  [P2] The walking view has no heading
  - Location: viewNav in web/app/index.html
  - Category: Accessibility, WCAG 2.4.6
  - Impact: It's the only view without an <h1>, so focus has nowhere to land
    when the walk starts. The instruction banner is a status region outside the
    panel.
  - Fix: Add an <h1> (it can be visually quiet), for example "Caminando a
    Monumento a la Madre".
  - Command: /impeccable harden

  [P2] "Deshacer" disappears after 6.5 seconds
  - Location: toast() in web/app/index.html
  - Category: Accessibility, WCAG 2.2.1
  - Impact: Deleting a report is only undoable through a toast that times out.
    Slower users lose the undo.
  - Fix: Keep action toasts open until dismissed, or until focus or hover leaves
    them.
  - Command: /impeccable harden

  [P3] Colors are duplicated in JS, and app type steps aren't in DESIGN.md
  - Location: Leaflet style objects in the app, canvas fills in the landing
    script, and the 13 off-ramp type sizes
  - Category: Theming / Integrity
  - Impact: Change a token and the map lines won't follow it.
  - Fix: Read the colors from CSS variables with getComputedStyle once at
    startup. Add an "App UI" type and color section to DESIGN.md (/impeccable
    document).
  - Command: /impeccable document

  [P3] Map markers aren't keyboard reachable, and the zoom buttons are 30px
  - Location: Leaflet layers in the app
  - Category: Accessibility / Responsive
  - Impact: Low. Every piece of marker information is also available in the
    panel, and on phones the zoom buttons are hidden in favor of pinch.
  - Command: /impeccable polish

  [P3] will-change: transform on four selectors at rest
  - Location: web/index.html:43 (web/index.html#L43)
  - Category: Performance
  - Impact: Extra compositing layers kept alive all the time.
  - Command: /impeccable polish

  Patterns

  - The app's accessibility gaps are all custom widgets without their keyboard
    contract: radio groups, a combobox, timed toasts. The basics are in place
    (labels, names, focus, contrast apart from the clay text).
  - Both pages hard-code palette values in JavaScript wherever a canvas or
    Leaflet needs a color.

  Positive findings

  - No unnamed controls in any state measured, and every image has alt text (the
    decorative closing photo correctly has alt="").
  - No horizontal overflow at 390px or with text at 200%, on both pages.
  - Tap targets on phones are at least 44px, apart from inline text links, which
    are exempt.
  - Native <dialog> for confirmations, focus moved to each view's heading, a
    polite status region for toasts, and errors tied to fields with
    aria-describedby.
  - Reduced motion is honored on the landing page as DESIGN.md specifies: the
    lens holds still, the walker is removed, and routes stay fully drawn.

  Recommended actions

  1. [P1] /impeccable optimize: serve the hero as the 489 KB JPEG, and pause the
     landing map loop when it's offscreen or under reduced motion.
  2. [P1] /impeccable harden:
     - set warning text in ink with a clay icon
     - roving tabindex on the four radio groups
     - the combobox pattern on Desde/Hasta
     - the panel as <main>
     - an <h1> on the walking view
     - persistent undo toasts
  3. [P3] /impeccable document: add the app's type steps and map colors to
     DESIGN.md, and read Leaflet colors from the CSS variables.
  4. /impeccable polish: a final pass, including the will-change cleanup.

  You can ask me to run these one at a time, all at once, or in any order you
  prefer.

  Re-run /impeccable audit after fixes to see your score improve.

● All the audit findings are fixed, committed, and pushed to origin/app-goia.
  The 7.3 MB hero image was the exception. That finding was wrong: the page
  already wraps the image in a <picture> with a JPEG <source>, and a network log
  confirmed browsers download only the 489 KB hero-photo.jpg. I left it
  unchanged.

  Results after the fixes: the detector reports 0 findings, in the source and in
  all four rendered scans (landing and app, desktop and phone). No text fails
  contrast. The full app flow still passes at both sizes with no script errors.

  What changed, each checked in the browser:
  - Warning contrast: the "Pasa cerca de una zona con reportes" text is now ink,
    with the icon kept in clay. DESIGN.md now says clay is for shapes and icons
    only.
  - Landing map: it now redraws only while on screen: 60 times a second in view,
    0 once scrolled away. Under reduced motion it redraws only when the pointer
    moves.
  - Route choices and other option groups: one Tab stop per group, and the arrow
    keys move the selection.
  - Desde/Hasta: they are now announced as search fields with a results list,
    the result count is read out, and ArrowDown moves into the results.
  - Page structure: the side panel is now the page's <main>, and the walking
    view has a heading that takes focus when the walk starts.
  - Undo after deleting a report: the message now stays until you press Deshacer
    or close it.
  - Type sizes and colors:
    - The app's text sizes are now a fixed scale of CSS variables, and DESIGN.md
      has a new "App UI scale" section plus two colors (fast-gray-deep,
      ink-mist-dim).
    - The map and canvas colors are read from the CSS tokens on both pages.
    - The landing's off-system radii and colors now use the tokens. That
      includes the three 0.45cqw radii, which were already about 6px, so nothing
      looks different.
  - Smaller fixes:
    - Sponsor pins can be reached by keyboard, and the zoom buttons are 40px.
    - The map credit has padding.
    - Paragraphs no longer end on a single word.
    - The landing no longer sets will-change permanently.

  Commits on app-goia:
  - c80d8f6: one combined application in web/.
  - fcdcaac: the audit fixes.

  .impeccable/build/) were left out of both commits. To open a pull request into
